戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

### Page 10

不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  7 August 2026   COMMODITIES, FOOD & AGRI
Could copper lose its tariff premium?
Copper has rallied sharply on expectations of US import tariffs, as traders
rush metal into the US and physical markets tighten. With prices back
near record highs, any policy disappointment could put that tariff
premium to the test
Monthly US copper imports surged to a 12-year high of more than 200,000 tonnes in July ahead of

potential tariffs
Copper is trading above $14,000/t, close to its record high. At the same time, LME inventories
have fallen further, and the cash-to-three-month spread has moved deeper into
backwardation, highlighting increasingly tight physical market conditions.
In our June note, What’s next for US copper import tariffs?, we examined the possible policy
outcomes. Since then, traders have continued to position for potential tariffs, with the impact

becoming increasingly visible in trade flows, inventories and physical market indicators.
Tariff expectations remain a key market driver
Copper shipments into the US have accelerated ahead of a potential tariff decision, pushing
COMEX inventories to a record high. US copper imports exceeded 200,000 tonnes in July alone
– the highest monthly level in at least 12 years.

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 7 August 2026 2
COMEX inventories reach record highs
Source: COMEX, ING Research
Wide COMEX-LME spread continues to attract copper into the
US
Source: COMEX, LME, ING Research
At the same time, the London copper market is showing increasing signs of tightness. LME
inventories have fallen to a five-month low, while the cash-to-three-month spread has
widened to around $120/t backwardation – up from about $40 a week ago and the widest

since October, pointing to a squeeze on short-term supplies.

### Page 3

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 7 August 2026 3
Nearby copper market tightens
Source: LME, ING Research
LME inventories continue to decline
Source: LME, ING Research
Can tariff expectations continue to drive prices?
The recent rally is not solely tariff-driven, however.
Mine supply growth remains constrained, while low treatment charges continue to point to
tight concentrate availability. Demand linked to electrification, power grid investment and AI

infrastructure also remains supportive. We continue to expect the global refined copper
market to record a deficit of around 35k tonnes in 2026.
Much of the recent rally reflects expectations that tariffs will be implemented broadly as
expected. But if the final measures are delayed, narrower than expected or exempt refined
copper, part of the recent rally could unwind. Stockpiling into the US would slow, inventory
flows would begin to normalise, and some of the current tightness outside the US would ease.

Any correction could be amplified if investors unwind positions built on tariff expectations.

### Page 4

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 7 August 2026 4
The tariff decision is only part of the story
The prospect of US tariffs has already reshaped the copper market. Inventory flows have
shifted, physical conditions have tightened, and prices have moved back towards record highs.
Much of that repricing has already happened. Once the tariff decision is announced, the
market's focus is likely to shift back to underlying fundamentals.
That would not necessarily change the broader outlook for copper. Any correction would be

aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from Refinitiv share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by Refinitiv.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from Refinitiv product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your Refinitiv sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

## Document 252: Rates Spark_ US long-end under increasing scrutiny

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ US long-end under increasing scrutiny.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 24 August 2026 1
THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  24 August 2026   RATES SPARK
Rates Spark: US long-end under
increasing scrutiny
US and EUR long-end yields remain under pressure from fiscal concerns,
resilient growth, energy risks and renewed supply, keeping bearish
steepening risks intact even as benign US inflation supports the front end
Long-end yields remain under pressure amid fiscal concerns
US: A bearish steepening theme is more likely to persist

The US long end will remain under scrutiny. The US Treasury’s attempt to dampen the rise
under the guise of scaled-up liquidity operations has only drawn more attention to the
underlying issue, the growing debt load and the limited ability or willingness to rein in the US
deficit. While it has been signalled that some announcements to cut back on wasteful
spending will be made in coming days, without a big rethink coming from Congress, we are
only looking at trimming the edges.

There are other factors aggravating the situation in bond markets, such as the tech-related
issuance (markets will be paying attention to this week’s earnings) and, on the monetary
policy side, the ongoing concern with inflation. On the latter, some of the heat has come off
though. The market will be looking to the Federal Reserve's favoured inflation measure, the
core PCE, this Wednesday. But the CPI release has already been benign alongside other

indicators, e.g. producer prices.

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 August 2026 2
That means Fed Chair Kevin Warsh might be under less pressure to provide the clarity the
market is seeking when he delivers his speech at this year’s Jackson Hole conference
on Friday. The market is down to discounting 10bp of tightening for September, which
translates into the implied probability of a hike of 40%. At the start of this month, we were
above 70%.
Overall, the front end might stay better supported, but the issues plaguing the long end are

not going away. We think this leaves a curve twisting steeper or even outright bearish
steepening theme intact as our base case. We had flagged last week that 10y US Treasury
yields could well test beyond the 4.75% towards 5%.
EUR: The long-end has been an issue, but there is no letup in
short-end pressure
Elevated deficits and high debt loads are not only a US issue, but something European bond
markets have grappled with for some time already. France and Italy are the main concern

currently, but even Germany is facing scrutiny with record-high issuance planned also in the
years ahead. 10y Bund yields have risen to above 3.25% over the past week, the highest since
2011, albeit in a less volatile fashion.
The underlying energy situation, with increasing concerns surrounding gas prices, remains a
more meaningful driver in the near term that keeps the market’s pricing of the European
Central Bank aggressive even beyond the September meeting. And we are likely to see an

energy-related rise in the flash CPIs coming from the individual countries this week, keeping
that pressure alive. On the growth side, the latest round of flash PMIs pointed to an economy
staying surprisingly resilient, offering the rates market little relief. We will be looking to
Germany’s Ifo release on Tuesday, but even here, the consensus is looking for a small
improvement.
A more technical driver that has perhaps played more into the timing of the rise in bond yields

is the resumption of issuance activities after the summer break. We have seen the first
government bond syndicated issuances, including a German 30y tap. But the broader SSA
market has also gained steam with German issuers testing the waters.
Monday’s events and market view
It will be a slow start to the week in terms of scheduled calendar events. We will be
mainly looking for headlines around the US deficit, with officials having touted coming
announcements to curb spending. On the geopolitical front, Treasury Secretary Scott

Bessent will provide more detail on US plans to increase the economic pressure on Iran
in a press conference today.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Reserve Bank of India policy committee 
(5 Aug)

The Reserve Bank of India monetary 
policy committee (MPC) is expected to 
keep the benchmark rate unchanged at 
5.25% on 5  Aug. We anticipate a 
cautious policy statement, that 
underscores the need for continued 
vigilance on the inflation outlook, while 
placing greater emphasis on core 
inflation as a more reliable measure of 
underlying price pressures than headline 
inflation, w hich has been influenced by 
both domestic and global supply -side 
factors. Against this backdrop, the 
committee is likely to push back a gainst 
market expectations of a more 
aggressive tightening path reflected in 
implied rates.

That said, the MPC is increasingly 
confronted by signs of a build -up in 
inflationary risks. Brent crude prices 
have risen amid renewed geopolitical 
tensions. Below-normal rainfall presents 
upside risks to food inflation, and the 
development of El Niño conditions could 
lead to higher imported agricultural 
commodity prices. Therefore, a more 
hawkish bias in policy guidance may 
become increasingly difficult to avoid as 
the real policy rate cushion narrows, 
inflation risks remain skewed to the 
upside, and the p robability of GDP 
growth exceeding 7% in 1QFY27 
increases.

For the path ahead,  markets will be 
watchful of developments in West Asia 
and signs of any change in the US 
Federal Reserve’s policy outlook in the 
second half of 2026. These 
developments will have important 
implications for the rupee, capital flows , 
and interest rate differentials. In the near 
term, the anticipated recovery in 
portfolio inflows, together with 
continued inflows via the swap windows, 
should provide a constructive backdrop 
for onshore markets.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

China

Exports growth is expected to moderate 
from 27.0% yoy in June to 17.0% in July, 
as renewed tensions in the Middle East 
weighed on external demand. High -
frequency indicators suggest export 
momentum has eased, with total 
deadweight tonnage handled at China's  
20 major ports slowing from 12.9% yoy in 
June to 12.1% in July. Meanwhile, 
international cargo flights declined 26.7% 
to an average of 9,468 per week in July, 
pointing to softer trade activity.

Hong Kong

Retail sales growth is expected to 
accelerate from 7.9% yoy in May to 8.5% 
in June, supported by resilient tourism 
activity and improving domestic 
consumption. Mainland visitor arrivals 
remained robust, growing 10.4% yoy in 
June. Meanwhile, the growth in residents' 
outbound departures eased from 8.1% 
yoy in May to 5.2% in June, suggesting 
fewer residents spending abroad and a

### Page 3

The Week Ahead July 31, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

partial shift in consumption back to the 
domestic market.

South Korea

July inflation data are due for release. 
Headline CPI is expected to remain 
elevated at 3.3% y oy, up slightly from 
3.2% in June and well above the Bank of 
Korea's 2% inflation target. The rebound 
in global oil prices, driven by renewed 
tensions in the Middle East, is likely to 
keep imported inflationary pressures 
elevated. Demand-side inflation remains 
relatively contained, reflecting the 
impact of the BOK's pre -emptive 
monetary tightening in anchoring 
inflation expectations.  We expect CPI 
inflation to rema in in the 3.0 –3.5% y oy 
range throughout 2H26, which should 
prompt the BOK to deliver an additional 
50bps of rate hikes, taking the policy 
rate to 3.25% by year-end.

Taiwan

July trade and inflation data are due for 
release. Export growth is expected to 
remain strong at around 40% y oy, 
resulting in a robust trade surplus of 
USD15bn. Excluding price effects, 
however, export growth is likely to have 
moderated to around 20% y oy. We 
maintain our view that the AI -driven 
export supercycle has peaked and is 
transitioning toward a more normali sed 
growth trajectory.

CPI inflation is expected to remain 
elevated at 2.5% y oy. The rebound in oil 
prices amid renewed Middle East 
tensions is likely to keep imported raw 
material costs elevated, while the severe 
typhoon in July is also expected to have 
pushed up fresh food prices. We 
continue to expect CPI inflation to 
remain in the 2.0 –2.5% y oy range 
through 2H26, prompting the central 
bank to deliver a 12.5bp s rate hike in 
4Q26.

Indonesia and Philippines

Inflation and 2Q26 growth numbers are 
due in early -August. For Indonesia, we 
expect 2Q26 growth to moderate to 5.3% 
yoy from 1Q’s 5.6%. Momentum at the 
beginning of the year was jumpstarted 
by religious festive spending and strong 
increase in public spending. Entering the 
second quarter, fiscal spending 
remained supportive, complemented by 
stimulus measures and the limited pass-
through of elevated global energy prices 
to household consumption, as domestic 
fuel prices remained unchanged. Private 
sector busin ess activity was likely 
impacted by Middle East hostilities, 
shifts in domestic policy architecture , 
and subdued trade sector performance.

Growth in Philippines is expected to 
show signs of bottoming out but remain 
at modest levels, with 2Q output set to 
register 3% yoy rise. Inflation outcomes 
will benefit from a temporary pullback in 
global energy prices, but food costs are 
set to turn dearer on El Nino risks in 
Philippines as well as Indonesia. BSP is 
expected to tighten policy rates, while BI 
will opt to keep rates on hold after back-
to-back increases.

### Page 4

The Week Ahead July 31, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to accelerate to 3.0% yoy in July, the 
upper end of the central bank’s 1 -3% 
target range, from 2.4% yoy in June. The 
pickup reflected firmer food price 
increases amid low base effects, and 
higher core inflation due t o the pass -
through of energy costs. Energy -driven 
inflation likely remained elevated as 
global and domestic fuel prices 
rebounded following the escalation of 
tensions in the Middle East. Nonetheless, 
the Bank of Thailand is unlikely to rush 
into monetary t ightening, as it looks 
through this supply -driven increase in 
inflation, balancing with concerns about 
uneven economic growth.

Vietnam

We expect Vietnam’s goods exports to 
expand by 25.5% yoy in July, extending 
the strong growth of 28.1% yoy in June, 
supported by robust electronics 
shipments benefitting from spillovers 
from artificial intelligence -related 
demand, despite weakness in texti les, 
garments, and footwear exports. Retail 
sales likely picked up amid resilient 
domestic consumption and solid tourism 
arrivals, despite elevated inflation. We 
anticipate headline inflation to rise to 5.0% 
yoy in July from 4.7% yoy in June, driven 
by fir mer transport price increases as 
global and domestic fuel prices 
rebounded following the escalation of 
tensions in the Middle East, alongside 
persistently elevated food inflation.

## Document 269: The World Should Not Ignore China’s Undervalued Currency (2026-08-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The World Should Not Ignore China’s Undervalued Currency (2026-08-02).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-08-02
Pages: 24
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
The World Should Not Ignore China’s
Undervalued Currency
A weak renminbi directly led to China’s latest export boom, and a stronger renminbi
is the only realistic cure.
Nurphoto/Getty
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
August 2, 2026 12:46 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
China’s current account surplus is now reported to be
around 4 percent of GDP—or 3 percentage points
higher than what the IMF estimates it should be.
That works out, using the IMF’s own calculations, to
an undervaluation of roughly 20 percent.
If adjustments are made for China’s surging gold
imports (1.5 percent of GDP in Q2) and its
obviously misreported investment income balance

(China reports a deficit of $125 billion on $4 trillion
in net assets, something that no one—the IMF
included—can explain) the current account surplus
would rise to around 6 percent of GDP and the
estimated undervaluation would increase to between
30 and 35 percent. The upward adjustment to
China’s surplus should not be controversial: the
customs surplus excluding gold is now an incredible
7% of China’s GDP, the services deficit is only 1
percent of GDP and a long history of surpluses

should lead to a positive net foreign asset position and
thus external investment income (as in Korea, Taiwan
and Japan).

### Page 3

3
China not only runs a large surplus. Its government,
rather obviously, controls the movement of its
currency. The People’s Bank of China (PBOC) sets
the daily midpoint for market trading and the state
commercial banks (perhaps acting on behalf of the
PBOC) buy and sell foreign exchange to keep the
yuan within that band. Recently they have been
buying foreign currency in size (something like $2
billion per trading day, $50 billion a month) to keep

the yuan from strengthening toward the upper edge
of the band. The yuan, in other words, goes where the
PBOC wants it to go.
That has been obvious for some time—and is
particularly obvious in the last few years. The PBOC
kept the “fix” (the midpoint for trading, notionally)
more or less constant from mid 2023 to mid 2025,
and since then the renminbi has been on a slow,
controlled, move up -- though it still has a way to go
to get back to its 2021 or early 2022 levels.

### Page 4

4
Moreover, China still has a largely closed financial
account. It effectively has chosen to limit financial
flows so as to allow both a stable (or controlled)
currency and domestic monetary policy autonomy.
The exchange rate isn’t set by interest rate
differentials and unconstrained financial flows, but
rather by the signals sent by China’s central bank and
the exporters’ incentive to convert the current account
surplus back into yuan.
That too has been obvious to close observers this past

year.* It explains why “settlement” (the net purchase
of foreign currency by the state financial system)
surged as the PBOC started to set stronger “fixes” and
the renminbi started to appreciate.
Given the extent of the undervaluation of the
renminbi and the scale of China’s surplus both
relative to its own economy and relative to the
economies of its trading partners, the most obvious

### Page 5

5
way China could bring its trade accounts into a bit
better balance would be to allow the renminbi to
strengthen. It currently is roughly were it was against
the dollar in 2008 -- and in inflation adjusted terms
against a basket of its trading partners, it is something
like 10% weaker than it was ten years ago -- and
China’s currency should have a trend appreciation so
the gap between its current level and where it should
be has become quite large.

Yet a collection of the world’s most distinguished
international economists—the former deputy
managing director of the IMF, the former chief
economist of the IMF and the incoming chief
economist of the Bank for International Settlements
—argue that the world shouldn’t press China to
change how it manages its currency.
They argue a mix of two things: one, that exchange
rate moves don’t have all that much of an impact, and

### Page 6

6
two, that pressure for a stronger RMB wouldn’t really
work even if China moved the exchange rate a bit,
because any nominal appreciation of the currency
would result in more deflation and thus no change to
the inflated-adjusted value of the yuan. The correct
policy, they argue, is to wait for China to conclude on
its own that it needs to do more to support domestic
demand, as stronger domestic demand growth will
eventually reflate the Chinese economy and generate

a real appreciation of the yuan.
That could mean waiting for an awfully long time.
The cost of waiting is not something these prominent
voices explicitly discuss, but it could be large.**
The status quo isn’t defined by a large but static
Chinese surplus. No, the status quo is one where
China relies on an increasing surplus to make up for
the ongoing weakness of its own internal economy.

### Page 7

7
Waiting for China to change thus means accepting
more Chinese exports, less production of traded goods
in the U.S., Europe, Japan, and India, and an
increased concentration of global industrial
production in China, with all the dependencies that
generates.
I offer five points in rebuttal of the arguments put
forward by Gopinath, Gourinchas, and Rey.
1. China’s currency management actually matters; it
isn’t secondary to other fundamentals
Back in 2022 and 2023, the renminbi depreciated by

10-15 percent against the dollar and by roughly half
as much against the basket.*** That nominal move
would not have happened without the tacit support of
the People’s Bank of China.

### Page 8

8
We know this because, once China wanted the move
to stop and the renminbi to stabilize, they held the fix
constant and the weak edge of the band constrained
the yuan’s movement (this is the period when the
banks were also net sellers of foreign currency, though
on a more modest scale than they are now net buyers).
At the time, China was experiencing deflation as a
consequence of its unresolved property crisis and Xi’s
unwillingness to stimulate household spending (he

preferred direct lending to support investment in
manufactures), and the U.S. and Europe were
experiencing significant inflationary pressures, so the
change in the real exchange rate was close to the
change in the nominal dollar-yuan exchange rate.
That depreciation should be assessed relative to an
appreciating trend, as Dr. Krugman has pointed out.
China’s convergence in manufacturing productivity
with the advanced economies should result in a real

appreciation. So depreciating rather than
appreciating has a large impact: the IMF’s standard
exchange rate model suggests a 10 percent move in
China’s currency should lead to a 1.5 percentage point
increase in net exports—and a closer to 15 percent
move relative to trend should result in an increase in
the contribution of net exports of more like 2
percentage points.
That simple forecast was more or less right. By the
end of 2023, 4-5 quarters after the big move in the

renminbi, Chinese export growth accelerated. That

### Page 9

9
acceleration then shows up as 1.5 pp contribution
from net exports to China’s growth in both 2024 and
2025.
This pattern, by the way, is entirely consistent with a
standard model where exchange rate changes do
matter, and they have their biggest impact with a lag.

### Page 10

10
Now, the modern IMF does at times seem to ignore
its own long-standing institutional wisdom on
balance of payments questions, for reasons that I don’t
fully understand. In 2022, 2023 and again in 2024,
the IMF’s official forecast for China showed that
China’s external surplus would shrink, not rise, over
time -- basically ignoring the real depreciation.
I will note that these forecasts have not been borne
out.
Based on the recent acceleration in China’s export

growth—and the similar export outperformance
during period of renminbi weakness after China
joined the WTO—it sure seems like a weakening
currency leads China’s exports to outperform global
trade. And there is every reason to think that a
stronger currency would lead China’s export growth to
decelerate to a pace that its trading partners can more
easily absorb.

### Page 11

11
Is “currency” the entire story of the last few years? No,
of course not. The weak currency worked off a bigger
manufacturing base thanks to China’s industrial
policies, which effectively raised the reward for
“macro” industrial policies. And China has long
rigged its domestic market against imports, and as
China’s indigenous technological base converged
toward the global frontier, those policies started to
impact a broader range of sectors.
At the same time, it is very hard to understand the

enormous acceleration in China’s export growth in
late 2023 and 2024 and the sustained
outperformance of China’s exports in 2025 without
some reference to moves in the renminbi (assessed
with standard lags).
2. Nominal exchange rate moves usually result in
real exchange rate moves
When inflation pushes the real exchange rate of a
deficit country with a more or less fixed exchange rate
up (gulp, Argentina; gulp, Egypt in the past), the
IMF doesn’t generally advocate keeping the currency

stable and deflating the economy to restore external
competitiveness.
Rather, it generally advises a depreciation of the
currency, because inflation doesn’t offset the full
impact of a nominal move and the effect of
depreciation on domestic prices can be offset with
other policies.

### Page 12

12
There is equally no strong basis for thinking deflation
would quickly offset the full impact of a nominal
appreciation in a country with a large and rising
surplus. Exchange rates can move quickly; domestic
prices adjust partially and with a long lag. There isn’t
an immediate offset.
Given global concerns about the immediate impact
of China’s export expansion, a nominal appreciation
thus offers immediate relief.
And if the nominal appreciation leads China to, well,

clean up its property sector (as the IMF has long
advised) and spend more on consumption (as the
IMF has only recently started to recommend) the
nominal appreciation need not be offset by future
deflation either.
This isn’t just theory. Solid (if old) empirical work has
long found that nominal exchange rate moves do
result in real exchange rate moves. Krugman
highlighted this back in 2011.
And there is no evidence that the nominal
depreciation of 2022-23 materially reduced the pace

of deflation in China, and also zero evidence that the
modest nominal appreciation of the last year led to a
faster pace of deflation. If anything the pace of
deflation has moderated, though I fully accept that
higher oil prices have had something to do with that.

### Page 13

13
3. Xi won’t change without a bit of external pressure
Gopinath, Gourinchas and Rey write:
“What China needs to do—and what is in its own long-term
interest, given its ageing population and an investment model
overly reliant on the tradable sector—is to raise the share of
household income in GDP, expand social insurance so that
families feel able to spend, and stop financing tradable-sector
expansion at the expense of consumption. Do those things,

and a real appreciation of the yuan will follow”
No disagreement from me. I would add that China
also needs to change its very regressive system of
taxation. See this blog.
I also don’t dispute that China’s unresolved property
crisis has an impact on the current account (even if it
didn’t show up immediately in the Chinese data
because of the misguided 2022 balance of payments
revisions) and falling property prices still weigh on
consumption.

### Page 14

14
But the property bubble burst in 2021. We are now
well into 2026. At some point, China needs to
resume growing off its own demand.
Net exports have already added six percentage points
to China’s growth and are on track to add a seventh
point in 2027. That is well over a percentage of world
GDP. In other words, the bursting of the bubble has
been a shock to the entire global economy, not just to
China. Ask Germany.
There is no sign that waiting for President Xi to see

the light will lead China to start writing big checks to
households any time soon. I also would not hold my
breath and wait for President Trump to recognize the
need to reduce the fiscal deficit. Waiting for a U.S.-
China deal on the “fundamentals” likely means
waiting for a long time. And that isn’t something that
Europe can really afford. Ask Chancellor Merz.
By contrast, what likely would happen if the renminbi
appreciated and China stopped getting 1 to 1.5 pp of

its annual growth from net exports?
My bet is that Xi would be forced to make a few hard
choices. He might need to reconsider his reported
belief that stimulating households directly is wasteful.
He might need to put a bit of pressure on the
Finance Ministry to run bigger fiscal deficits to
support upfront reform to raise demand. My guess is
that Chinese policymakers would end up doing a bit
of needed household stimulus – to their own benefit

### Page 15

15
and to that of the world. The Gopinath, Gourinchas
and Rey recommendation, by contrast, risks being a
recipe for continued inaction.
4. Exchange rate diplomacy has a better record than
is commonly assumed
The Plaza Accords, contrary to the arguments made
by the G7 economists, did actually bring down the
overall U.S. trade deficit as well as Japan’s surplus. It
took a couple of years, but that is because exchange
rates have an impact with a lag. The adjustment in

the Deutsche Mark, incidentally, also brought down
Germany’s surplus.
And the common belief (especially in China) that the
Plaza accord led to Japan’s bubble economy doesn’t
stand up to scrutiny. Germany let its currency
appreciate too and didn’t have a property bubble. And
we have learned in the years since that there are many
paths to a bubble economy that don’t involve a
currency appreciation:: the U.S. in the naughty
aughties, Spain before 2009, China 2015-2020.

Moreovef, China has responded to currency pressure
in the past. It took way too long, but China did let the
yuan move in 2007 anbbbd 2008. And after
repegging in the crisis and getting stuck at 6.28 for a
couple of years during the global financial crisis,
China opted for renewed appreciation in 2011 rather
than risk legislation that would result in across the
board tariffs (Schumer-Graham).

### Page 16

16
Putting a bit of pressure on China to end its current
reliance on net exports for an acceptable pace of
growth thus seems like an acceptable risk, and far
better (at least from Europe’s point of view), than
continuing to pay a price for remaining mostly open
to China at a time when the yuan is clearly deeply
undervalued. Chinese cars seem very cheap, just as
pizzas in Argentina now seem expensive; see the big
Mac index -- though ignore the Economist’s leader!

Gopinath, Gourinchas, and Rey are reluctant to
prioritize pressing China to make policy choices that
would move China towards external balance (a
smaller surplus) if they put China’s internal balance in
question. And they—like the IMF itself—are
reluctant to call on China to do a big sustained fiscal
expansion, which is the one policy that classic
Keynesian analysis would say moves China toward
both external and internal balance.

### Page 17

17
In fact, for most of the last five years, the IMF
encouraged China to combine fiscal tightening with
monetary easing—a policy that would have (maybe)
addressed deflation (fiscal tightening doesn’t help
there, and there is a debate about the effectiveness of
monetary policy without fiscal support) while clearly
pushing China towards an even larger external
surplus.
5 Addressing China’s imbalances without a stronger
nominal RMB would take a massive reflation, one

far bigger than anything the IMF now supports
China’s surplus is no longer small. The goods surplus
is 7 percent of GDP if gold imports (effectively an
import of a financial asset) are excluded. Honestly
measured, without adjustments from an internal
survey that cannot be independently assessed, the
goods and services surplus is now 6 percent of GDP.
The current account should be similar or bigger given
China’s net foreign asset position (don’t trust me?

Look at the coefficient on net foreign assets in the
IMF’s own model and apply it to China’s 20% of
GDP net foreign asset position). Even using China’s
reported 4 percent of GDP surplus (give or take, with
a forecast for q2 2026), the undervaluation is now
more than 20 percent -- and since China’s real
exchange rate needs to appreciate to offset ongoing
technological catch up, the needed more over time is
even bigger.

### Page 18

18
Getting that kind of adjustment through domestic
policy shifts alone, without an assist from a stronger
set of fixes from the PBOC, would take a massive
swing in policy.
Yet the IMF’s actual recommendations for China are
incredibly timid . The proposed 0.5 pp of GDP,
“temporary” fiscal expansion to support consumption
might knock 0.2 pp of GDP off the external account
in a standard model. The Fund wants China to scale
back the subsidies that have pumped up industrial

investment. In a simple model that would actually
increase the external surplus not reduce, as any
reduction in investment relative to savings implies a
larger surplus (the steel that has gone into building
factories would need to be exported ... ). The Fund
argues that this wouldn’t happen, because China’s
households would recognize that inefficient
investment reduces their future wealth and thus
would anticipate higher future income from a more

efficient and start spending those gains today. Maybe,

### Page 19

19
but I personally wouldn’t bet on it. Cutting inefficient
property investment didn’t magically spur household
consumption over the last five years.
A true reflation of China’s economy would take a
much more aggressive fiscal expansion financed by
the central government (which doesn’t have much net
debt actually) than the Fund has been willing to call
for -- and of course what really matters is what Xi
does, not what the Fund recommends.**** I am all for

expanding social insurance, but if expanded benefits
are financed only by expanding the already very
regressive set of social contributions the immediate
impact on consumption won’t be large. There needs
to be a fiscal bridge, and I would say a big one, to a
more generous system of benefits that is financed
much more heavily out of income tax receipts.******
If Gopinath, Gourinchas and Rey don’t believe
exchange rate moves have much of an impact and

should not be a priority now, the scale of the needed
shift to other policies needs to be ramped up to be
commensurate with the true size of China’s surplus
The former Deputy Governor of the Banque de
France, Jean-Pierre Landau and a member of France’s
Council of Economist Analysis, Sebastian Jean, have

### Page 20

20
noted that a weak real exchange rate is a core feature
of Xi’s China, and not just a by product of the current
cyclical downturn. It supports the industrial and
technological upgrading that Xi wants, helps
subsidized industrial sectors gain the global scale they
need, creates incentives for the import substitution Xi
wants and allows Xi to get the growth he needs
without sending out the checks to households that he
believes to be inefficient. When lots of money wants

to leave China, the desired real exchange rate emerges
naturally -- and when the inflow from the trade
surplus exceeds the allowed outflows (as is the case
now), China has no problem asking the state banks to
warehouse the extra foreign exchange. A close
examination of the pattern of settlement shows that it
sure still acts like an intervention variable even if the
proceeds from settlement no longer all appear on the
PBOC’s balance sheet.
Convincing Xi to change his current policy settings

won’t be easy -- that applies as much to changing
China’s system of social insurance and welfare
payments as to changing China’s currency
preferences. Progress on either front may well take a
bit of real pressure -- which probably now needs to
come from Europe rather than a distracted United
States.
But there is a growing sense that something does
need to change. It isn’t just that China’s $1.4 trillion
goods surplus (without gold imports) which is almost

three times bigger than China’s pre-global financial

### Page 21

21
crisis surplus when scaled to the GDP of China’s
trading partners. Now it is just that China is set to
export10 million plus vehicle exports a year. It is that
China’s current model is based on an ever rising
surplus. Growing by net exports requires an
expanding trade surplus. China has already gotten 6
percentage points of growth from net exports over the
last 6 years, a total that will rise to 7 percentage points
at the end of this year. That implies an ever bigger

China shock, and ever more dependence on China for
key industrial inputs. China is now on a trajectory
that would lead it to export 20 million cars (roughly 1
in 3 of all cars sold outside China) in as few as three
more years. Similarly shifts are possible in a range of
other strategic industries.
Is inaction really still an option? And if not exchange
rate adjustment, a policy change that is known to
work, then what?
* The IMF has not invested heavily in close

observation of China’s exchange rate management;
look at the discussions of China’s currency and the
PBOC’s activities in the last few staff reports (in the
2025 staff report, paragraph 36 and figure 3) and
compare them to my blogs on the issue. In the past
few years, I have not seen any serious discussion of
state bank flows, FX settlement, the diversified use of
China’s foreign exchange reserves (see Box 6 of
SAFE’s 2020 annual report), SAFE’s co-investment

### Page 22

22
funds, SAFE’s entrusted loans, or the PBOC balance
sheet’s missing external interest income.
** Tooze would say that the stakes of China’s
industrial policy shock are “nothing less than a
complete transformation of industrial division of
labour at world scale”
***If I understand Dr. Gopinath’s past work correctly,
the move against the dollar (“dominant currency
pricing”) may matter more than the move against the
basket.
**** I do have some sympathy for the IMF here.

China is both big, and extremely unbalanced. The
IMF’s standard play book isn’t designed for a country
that saves over 40% of its GDP -- or for a country
that combines ruthless competition and state
guidance in new ways. Look at China’s fiscal
numbers, and a standard model would forecast that
the country would be running a current account
deficit and face inflationary pressures. Conversely, the
standard medicine for a country with deflation and
an “excessive” current account surplus is a fiscal

expansion, especially if monetary transmission is
gummed by a weak banking system. No wonder the
actual policy recommendations have often been
confused.
***** My only significant point of disagreement with
Michael Pettis is that he emphasizes the growth
China’s overall debt and I would emphasize the debt

### Page 23

23
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

CHINA ECONOMICS
of China’s central government, which remains
exceptionally low compared to other large economies.
Indeed, an important 2023 IMF working paper has
shown the central government doesn’t actually have
any net debt, given its substantial financial assets (the
domestic side of Huijin, SASAC, etc). I do agree
with Pettis that any realistic adjustment requires
China accepting a period of slower headline growth,
the central government’s fiscal space just needs to be

used offset the loss of the impetus to growth from net
exports and the drag from slower investment during a
period of adjustment.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
2
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has posted a sustained
improvement since mid-July, rising 2.6pts
to 159.6, a new historical high.
• Quarterly growth momentum has
lifted back into the 1.1–1.5% range,
the strongest pace since late-March.

Notably, this has little to do with the
move higher in fuel prices following
the end of temporary excise tax cuts,
with this effect having little bearing on
quarterly growth rates so far.
• Monthly growth momentum is also
holding steady at around 0.7%, although
here the impact of fuel price changes is
clearer with card activity ex fuel tracking
a softer 0.3% pace.
• The softer fuel and electricity spending
over the quarter appears to have freed up

some capacity for discretionary spending.
Discretionary segments are now growing
at 2.4%, the strongest pace in around
a year. Much of the lift has been driven
by holiday travel, which has rebounded
following disruptions earlier in the year
associated with the Middle East conflict.
In contrast, spending on essentials has
been broadly flat over the quarter.
• By state, Victoria continues to lead the
majors, with card spending up 2.4%,
although momentum has softened

noticeably over the past four weeks.
Elsewhere, spending growth remains
more modest but positive, tracking
between 1.1% in NSW and 1.8% in WA.
• Momentum has strengthened through
recent months, even though consumers
face several headwinds. Looking back,
the Q2 Household Spending Indicator,
which captures around two-thirds of
consumption, recorded a solid 0.7% rise
in real terms. However, we still expect
the broader national accounts measure
to show a more modest 0.2% increase in

Q2 when it is released on 2 September.
While still early in the quarter, the latest
card data suggest some upside risk to
our current forecast of a 0.4% rise in
household consumption in Q3. Overall,
we expect consumption to rise by 1.6%
in 2026 (see our August Market Outlook
for the full outlook and our August Red
Book for a consumer deep dive).
“.... essentials relief
supporting discretionary
spending.”

1. Westpac–DataX Card Tracker Index*

Card activity: solid growth pulse returns
The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker presents indicators based on the millions of
credit and debit card transactions processed by Westpac every day. The measures
are a timely guide to shifts in spending. See p 9 for a detailed explanation.
This report is produced by Westpac Economics.
Matthew Hassan, Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
Neha Sharma, Economist
Email: economics@westpac.com.au
This issue was finalised on 17 August 2026.

If you would like more insights on your sector or business from this and other
Westpac data please visit Westpac DataX or contact datax@westpac.com.au.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
3
• Chart 2 shows how quarterly growth
in the Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index compares to growth in nominal
consumption as reported in the quarterly
national accounts. All estimates are
adjusted for regular seasonal variations.
• The quarterly growth momentum

continued to improve since our last
update, with the latest pace tracking
1.5% – the strongest since late-
March. Underlying this is a 1.2% rise in
domestic transactions and a 6.0% rise in
international. Earlier estimates have also
been revised higher.
• Notably, excluding fuel and electricity
spending from this measure, quarterly
growth momentum was running at
2.1%, the strongest in around a year. The
near-term momentum tells a different
story, though, with broader card activity

tracking 0.7% over the past month and
0.3% after excluding electricity and fuel.
• Overall, we see some upside risk to our
current estimate of 0.4% for consumption
in Q3.
• The Q2 ABS household spending
indicator posted a solid 0.7% rise in real
terms. The full consumption wash-up
will be provided in the national accounts,
due September 2. We expect a 0.2% rise
in consumption over Q2.
Higher rates not biting the consumer

### Page 4

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
4
• Chart 4 shows quarterly growth in
card activity across broad categories.
The slowdown in Q2 was fairly broad-
based but slightly more pronounced for
essentials (noting that for essential goods
this is partly due to the unwinding fuel
price spike).

• The recent firming continues to be
centred on discretionary categories.
Growth in discretionary services is
running at 3.4%qtr (strongest since mid-
2025), in particular holiday travel which is
growing at 5.2%qtr. Discretionary goods
are growing at 1.2%qtr – the strongest in
about five months.
• By contrast spending on essentials
has been flat, with a modest rise in
services (+0.2%qtr) offset by a decline in
goods (–0.2%qtr). The weakness in the

latter is entirely due to vehicle-related
spending. The near-term momentum for
both appears much stronger, running
at 1.0%mth and 1.9%mth respectively,
mostly due to the operation of vehicles
and transport services component.
• Chart 5 shows card activity across
the major states. Vic continues to run
ahead of other states, with spending
up 2.4%qtr, though the trend softened
over the past four weeks. Card activity is
running at 1.1%qtr in NSW, 1.2%qtr in

Qld, 1.3%qtr in SA and 1.8%qtr in WA.
Discretionary categories and Vic lead

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 5
• Our MHSI and retail card indexes are 
composites based on transactions in 
categories that are in scope for the ABS 
monthly household spending indicator 
and ABS retail sales surveys (based on 
MHSI) respectively. 
• The divergence between the broader 
WCTI and the retail and MHSI card 
measures has narrowed substantially 
since our last update. The retail card 
index is now growing in line with 
broader spending, at 1.5%qtr. The recent 
slowing here is attributed to food-
related category, in particular hospitality 
spending, with non-food retail spend 
partly offsetting. Meanwhile the MSHI 
card index is well behind, at 0.9%qtr.
• The latest ABS MHSI showed a 0.8% 
rise in June, with quarterly growth lifting 
to 1.3%. This compares to the June 
monthly outcome for our index which 
was flat. The ABS attributed the recent 
strength in spending to electric vehicle 
purchases. This may explain some of the 
miss as vehicle purchases are not well-
captured in card transaction data alone. 
• Our MHSI card index rose 0.9% in July, 
with the official ABS outcome due 27 
August.
Food retailing slows led by hospitality

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS 9
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

## Document 286: “拯救美债”接力棒：贝森特上周搞砸了，本周看沃什了

Source PDF: all report pdf/“拯救美债”接力棒：贝森特上周搞砸了，本周看沃什了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-08-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⻉森特扩⼤⻓债回购未能有效压制美债收益率，反⽽催⽣⻩⾦和⽐特币的 “ 货币贬值交易 ” 。
市场焦点转向美联储主席沃什的杰克逊霍尔讲话 —— 若其⽆法就通胀路径给出明确信号，
⻓端美债抛售或将加剧。 30 年期收益率 5% 是关键关⼝，沃什表态将是本周最重要变量。
摘要：
⻉森特出⼿救债市，结果救了⻩⾦和⽐特币 —— 现在，所有⼈都在等沃什。
上周，美国财政部⻓⻉森特宣布将⾄少翻倍扩⼤⻓期美债回购规模，试图压制持续攀升的⻓端
收益率。效果⽴竿⻅影，但只持续了不到⼀天 —— 收益率随后重返⾼位，整周基本持平。
⻉森特事后接受采访时称市场 " 有点过度反应 " ，并强调财政部拥有 " 强⼤的⼯具箱 " 。但市场⽤⾏
动给出了不同的答案：美元当周下跌近 1% ，⻩⾦突破 4600 美元，⽐特币单周涨超 25% 。
这⼀组合被野村证券的 Charlie McElligott 定性为 " 压⼒释放阀 "—— 当局试图稳住⻓端利率，市场
的焦虑情绪转⽽在别处宣泄。
本周焦点：沃什能否给出答案
接⼒棒现在传到了美联储主席沃什⼿中。他将于本周五在杰克逊霍尔经济政策研讨会发表讲

话。
沃什⾃今年 5 ⽉就任以来，⼏乎未提供任何前瞻指引。上次 FOMC 会后的发⾔，直接引发了⼀轮
债市⼤跌 —— 市场对他说什么、怎么说，极度敏感。
据彭博报道，交易员最想知道的是：⾯对顽固⾼于 2% ⽬标的通胀，以及不断恶化的财政状况，
美联储的政策反应函数究竟是什么。
TD Securities 美国利率策略师 Molly Brooks 警告： " 如果还是⽼⼀套，我认为市场会感到失望，
这可能会加剧我们已经看到的⻓端抛售。 "
汇丰利率策略师 Dhiraj Narula 认为，沃什有机会通过表态来安抚市场： " 如果沃什主席能对潜在
通胀压⼒有所定性，在我们看来，这已经⾜以为降低不确定性相关的期限溢价提供⼀定依据。 "
彭博 Markets Live 策略师 Michael Ball 表⽰：⻉森特能调整债务期限结构，但只有美联储才能锚
定通胀预期。沃什的杰克逊霍尔讲话必须重申 2% ⽬标仍然可以实现，并明确表态 —— 如果通胀
持续，即便与⾏政当局产⽣摩擦，也将采取政策⾏动。
⻉森特的操作，为什么不够⽤？

Academy Securities 的 Peter Tchir 指出，⽬前美国政府有 7.5 万亿美元短期国库券（ T-bills ）和
21.7 万亿美元附息债券在外流通。⻉森特每次回购操作 " ⾄少 40 亿美元 " ，频率接近每周⼀次 ——
从之前的每次 20 亿翻倍到 40 亿，听起来声势不⼩，但根本没能持续撼动市场。
Tchir 判断，这不是 QE （量化宽松）。⻉森特的操作本质上只是在 " 重新排列甲板上的椅⼦ " ，并没
有真正创造货币。⻩⾦涨、美元跌的市场反应，更多反映的是对 " 货币贬值 " 叙事的过度解读，⽽
⾮财政部真正扩张了货币供给。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

还有⼀个鲜为⼈知但极为关键的数据：美联储⽬前持有所有 10 ⾄ 15 年到期美债的 50% 以上。这
距离 " ⾃由市场 " 已经相当遥远。与此同时，美联储持有的⻓期债券⽐例也接近 20% 。
更吊诡的是，美联储⼿中还持有近 4260 亿美元、将在⼀年内到期的附息债券，平均票息仅
2.9% ，⽽当前联邦基⾦有效利率为 3.63%—— 美联储正在这笔持仓上持续亏损利差。
“ 扭曲操作 ” ：⻉森特救不了的，美联储能救吗？
这⼀背景，让市场开始热议⼀个沉寂已久的⼯具：美联储版 " 扭曲操作 " （ Operation Twist ）。
逻辑并不复杂：如果美联储将那 4260 亿美元短期债券卖出，转⽽买⼊相同名义规模的 20 年期以
上⻓债，虽然会产⽣⼀笔前期账⾯亏损，但可以获得可观的利差收益（约 5.25% 的持有收益对⽐
3.63% 的资⾦成本）。更重要的是，这将吸收超过 20 年期以上债券流通量的 15% ，有效压低⻓端
收益率。
从沃什的⻆度看， " 扭曲操作 " 不算 QE ，因为它不改变美联储持有债券的总名义规模。这在政治

上更容易被接受。 Tchir 的判断是：如果⽩宫真的希望⻓端收益率下⾏，就必须放弃在⻉森特可
控范围内 " ⼩打⼩闹 " ，转⽽推动美联储全⼒介⼊扭曲操作。
彭博的分析师的 Ball 也持相似判断：⻉森特的⽅案越来越像⼀个 " 轻量版扭曲操作 "—— 财政部通
过回购退出⻓久期债务，转向短券和短息票；美联储则通过准备⾦管理购买短券，在不扩表的
前提下吸收前端供给。但这套组合拳有其内在⽭盾：短期融资占⽐越⾼，财政部对政策利率的
敞⼝就越⼤。⼀旦通胀迫使美联储加息，利息成本将以更快速度重置；⽽如果美联储因顾虑财
政成本⽽迟疑，市场则会以更⾼的期限溢价来惩罚其独⽴性。
因此，要么美联储出⼿⽀持⻉森特，要么这次⼲预将以失败告终 —— ⽽失败的⼲预，往往⽐不
⼲预危害更⼤。
数据窗⼝：周三 PCE 先⾏探路
在杰克逊霍尔讲话之前，市场还将迎来⼀个重要数据节点 —— 周三公布的 7 ⽉个⼈消费⽀出
（ PCE ）指数。
据彭博报道，过去⼀个⽉，通胀、就业和零售销售数据均在预期之内或低于预期，推动交易员

下调了近期加息预期。 PCE 数据若延续这⼀趋势，或将为沃什的讲话提供⼀定缓冲空间。
不过，时间窗⼝正在收窄。彭博分析指出，中期选举政治压⼒，加上美国经济分析局将于 9 ⽉底
更新 PCE 统计⽅法，都可能使 9 ⽉ FOMC 会议后的紧缩操作在政治观感上愈发复杂。
5% 是关键，贬值交易能否持续存疑
华尔街⻅闻写道，美银策略师 Michael Hartnett 将 30 年期美债收益率 5% 视为重要分界线，认为若
⽆法跌破，将加剧美元及⾼杠杆领域 —— 包括 AI 超⼤规模算⼒企业和私⼈信贷 —— 所承受的压
⼒。
桥⽔基⾦创始⼈ Ray Dalio 上周五发出警告，建议投资者削减债券敞⼝，持有⻩⾦和部分⽐特
币，以防范潜在的美国债务危机。
这⼀压⼒并⾮空⽳来⻛。彭博指出，随着伊朗冲突持续发酵，财政前景愈发受到市场关注；与
此同时， AI 相关企业债券发⾏激增，正与美国国债争夺同⼀批资本；外国投资者对美债的需求
也愈发 " 价格敏感 " ，对当前政策⾛向的容忍度也在下降。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

升息機率仍存，短期走勢震盪
分析師／謝孟璇
研究範疇／公用事業/汽車/科技
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《通膨壓力仍存，升息機會打壓公用事業估值》
美國商務部在 7/30公布美國第二季 GDP成長率為
1.5%、低於市場預期的 1.8%，也較第一季的 2.1%
有所放緩。儘管如此，通膨壓力仍居高不下 ，聯準
會偏好的「個人消費支出物價指數 」(PCE)在6月年
增3.7%，雖略低於5月的4.1%，但仍遠高於聯準會
設定的 2%目標。美國聯準會在 7/29的決策會議維
持目前基準利率不變 ，然而仍有三位地區主席持不
同意見，主張應升息以進一步抑制通膨 。目前市場
預期今年年底前聯準會將升息 0~2碼不等，升息機
會的預期仍對公用事業估值造成打擊 ，因發債融資

的利息負擔將直接侵蝕淨利潤。
另外，過去一、兩年市場為電力公用事業公司注入
AI溢價，導致板塊估值偏高 ，未來若升息機會上升 ，
估值溢價將面臨修正 ，短期內預估公用事業股價較
為震盪，對3Q26持中立看法。

重點摘要
• 紐 約 州 宣 布 針 對 耗 電 量 達
50MW以上的資料中心實施最
長一年的建設禁令。
• 緬因州 、德拉瓦州 、密西根州 、
賓州都曾提出資料中心暫停審
查法案。
• AI資料中心電力需求仍是近期
公用事業2Q26財報重點。
《紐約州州長簽署資料中心建設禁令》
紐約州州長霍楚(Kathy Hochul)在7/14簽署行政命
令，宣布針對耗電量達 50MW以上的大型資料中心
實施最長一年的建設禁令 ，並暫停核發相關環境許
可。此政策除了影響科技巨頭對資料中心的布局 ，
也對各地公用事業公司帶來不同衝擊 。對紐約州電
力公司而言 ，短期內可減緩因大型資料中心接入而
產生的輸配電建設壓力 ，降低電網擴充及供電穩定

性的風險 ，同時避免一般用戶承擔部分基礎設施投
資成本。然而，資料中心原是未來電力需求成長的
重要來源 ，禁令可能導致公用事業公司失去潛在售
電收入與長期成長機會 。另一方面 ，德州、維吉尼
亞州及其他對資料中心較友善的地區 ，可能因此吸
引更多AI與雲端服務業者投資。
紐約州的作法已引起其他州關注 ，緬因州、德拉瓦
州、密西根州 、賓州都曾提出資料中心禁建或暫停
審查法案 ，而維吉尼亞州則研議加強新案管制與電
網接入條件 。未來若更多州跟進 ，AI產業投資可能
轉向德州等監管較寬鬆地區 ，並重新改變美國資料
中心與公用事業產業的發展版圖。
《AI資料中心電力需求仍為近期財報重點》
綜觀近期公用事業2Q26財報重點，AI資料中心電力
需求仍是共同議題。新紀元能源(NEE)表示，隨著電
力需求加速成長 ，旗下佛州電力 (FPL)已將2032年
大型負載需求預估由 6GW上調至 8GW，目前更有
約12GW資料中心相關需求進入深入洽談階段 。為
支應未來供電需求 ，FPL第二季資本支出約 28億美

元，全年資本投資預估達120至130億美元。美國南
方公司(SO)則持續受惠於美國東南部資料中心與製
造業投資成長 ，管理層強調將透過電網升級 、儲能
及發電資產擴建來滿足新增負載需求 。公司目前維
持約810億美元長期資本支出計畫 ，以強化供電可
靠度並支援未來大型用電客戶成長。

摘要 
“投资于人”的本质是以人的全面发展为目标进行资源配置， “投资于物”以
及其他形式的资源投入都要服务于人的发展需求。 “投资于人” 是经济发展阶
段变化以及顺应人的需求变化的必然要求，对于供给、分配、消费三大环节
都具有重要意义，是推动经济高质量发展的重要基础。本文概括分析了“投
资于人”的四大基本要求：服务于人的基本需要、服务于人的升级需求、服
务于人的能力建设、服务于人的自由流动。围绕上述要求，本文重点分析了
“投资于人”的重点任务，包括补齐民生保障短板、推动服务业扩容、提升
人力资本质量、优化资源配置的空间布局，并据此提出了相应的政策建议。
